115 年 10 月海關進出口貿易初步統計 
一、進出口貿易變動 
（一）出口 
10 月出口 967.6 億美元，創歷年單月新高，較上月增 10.9%，較上
年同月增 58.9%(按新臺幣計算增 66.6%)；累 計 1 至 10 月出口 7,582.9
億美元，較上年同期增 47.7%(按新臺幣計算增 51.1%)。 
（二）進口 
10 月進口 688.9 億美元，亦創歷年單月新高，較上月增 8.3%，較上
年同月增 75.1%(按新臺幣計算增 83.5%)；累 計 1 至 10 月進口 5,697.6
億美元，較上年同期增 44.6%(按新臺幣計算增 47.6%)。 
（三）出入超 
10 月出超 278.8 億美元，為單月新高，較上年同月增 63.3 億美元；
累計 1 至 10 月出超 1,885.4 億美元，較上年同期增 689.7 億美元。

二、出口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
受惠於人工智慧(AI)、高效能運算及雲端服務等應用商機持續拓展，
加上適逢傳統外銷旺季，以及價格攀升效應，10 月出口較上年同月
增 358.7 億美元(+58.9%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 資通與視聽產品出口 476.0 億美元(歷年單月新高)，較上年同月增
200.7 億美元(+72.9%)，其中電腦及其附屬單元增 151.6 億美元
(+67.5%)、電腦之零附件增 18.5 億美元(+1.0 倍)、儲存媒體增 17.3
億美元(+2.1 倍)、交換器及路由器增 11.7 億美元(+1.3 倍)。 
2. 電子零組件出口 326.8 億美元(歷年單月新高)，較上年同月增 124.3
億美元(+61.4%)，其中積體電路增 120.5 億美元(+63.0%)、印刷電
路增 1.9 億美元(+38.5%)、電容器及電阻器增 1.2 億美元(+48.6%)。 
3. 基本金屬及其製品出口 28.8 億美元，較上年同月增 7.6 億美元
(+35.7%)，其中銅及其製品增 4.7 億美元(+1.2 倍)、鋼鐵及其製品
增 1.9 億美元(+16.4%)。 
4. 機械出口 28.8 億美元(歷年單月新高)，較上年同月增 7.5 億美元
(+35.5%)，其中生產半導體等機械增 2.9 億美元(+57.5%)、機器、
工廠或實驗室設備增 1.4 億美元(+70.0%)。 
5. 礦產品出口 15.5 億美元，較上年同月增 6.5 億美元(+71.6%)，其
中石油煉製品增 5.7 億美元(+72.2%)。

（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 10 月出口較上年同期增 2,448.3 億美元(+47.7%)，以資通
與視聽產品增 1,423.3 億美元(+72.5%)、電子零組件增 850.5 億美元
(+47.5%)增加較多，礦產品、機械亦各增 41.9 億美元(+36.8%)、41.5
億美元(+19.8%)。

生產等出現結構性轉變，爰另予併計。

三、進口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
隨 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口衍生需求之帶動，積體電路、資通產品
及半導體設備進口持續強勁，加上國際原物料行情居高，10 月總進
口較上年同月增 295.5 億美元(+75.1%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 農工原料進口 458.3 億美元，較上年同月增 195.0 億美元(+74.0%)，
其中電子零組件增 136.1 億美元(+1.1 倍)、礦產品增 22.8 億美元
(+58.0%)、電腦之零附件增 16.2 億美元(+70.0%)、基本金屬增 6.2
億美元(+33.4%)、塑化原料增 5.0 億美元(+22.0%)。 
2. 資本設備進口 163.4 億美元，較上年同月增 84.1 億美元(+1.1 倍)，
其中資通設備增 46.9 億美元(+2.0 倍)、半導體設備增 22.4 億美元
(+83.4%)、交通運輸設備增 7.6 億美元(+7.0 倍)。 
3. 消費品進口 61.1 億美元，較上年同月增 18.8 億美元(+44.5%)，其
中固態非揮發性儲存裝置增 16.0 億美元(+5.3 倍)、食品增 0.5 億
美元(+5.1%)、小客車增 0.4 億美元(+10.2%)。

四、主要出口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
10 月對美國、歐洲、日本出口創新高 
1. 對美國出口 329.3 億美元，較上年同月增 118.1 億美元(+55.9%)，
其中資通與視聽產品增 98.3 億美元(+56.6%)、電子零組件增 13.5
億美元(+1.7 倍)、機械增 2.4 億美元(+41.6%)。 
2. 對中國大陸與香港出口 204.4 億美元，較上年同月增 61.4 億美元
(+42.9%)，其中電子零組件增 47.4 億美元(+48.0%)、資通與視聽
產品增 5.6 億美元(+36.5%)、基本金屬及其製品增 3.2 億美元
(+95.2%)。 
3. 對東協出口 184.6 億美元，較上年同月增 84.1 億美元(+83.8%)，
其中電子零組件增 38.6 億美元(+75.5%)、資通與視聽產品增 36.5
億美元(+1.4 倍)、礦產品增 5.0 億美元(+2.4 倍)。 
4. 對歐洲出口 59.5 億美元，較上年同月增 18.7 億美元(+46.0%)，其
中資通與視聽產品增 10.7 億美元(+47.9%)、電子零組件增 3.3 億
美元(+81.3%)、機械增 1.2 億美元(+50.8%)。 
5. 對日本出口 36.7 億美元，較上年同月增 12.2 億美元(+49.6%)，其
中電子零組件增 7.5 億美元(+63.2%)、資通與視聽產品增 3.2 億美
元(+1.3 倍)、化學品增 0.5 億美元(+47.0%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 10 月對美國出口 2,407.1 億美元，較上年同期增 891.1 億美
元(+58.8%)，對中國大陸與香港出口 1,805.3 億美元，增 416.6 億美
元(+30.0%)，對東協出口 1,524.1 億美元，增 547.6 億美元(+56.1%)，
對歐洲出口 481.3 億美元，增 157.1 億美元(+48.5%)，對日本出口 314.4
億美元，增 70.5 億美元(+28.9%)。

五、主要進口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
10 月自東協進口 134.1 億美元，較上年同月增 82.3 億美元(+1.6 倍)，
自南韓進口 130.0 億 美 元，增 73.1 億美元(+1.3 倍)，自中國大陸與香
港進口 128.3 億美元，增 51.9 億美元(+67.9%)，自日本進口 62.0 億
美元，增 18.8 億美元(+43.4%)，自美國進口 53.6 億美元，增 14.7 億
美元(+37.9%)，自歐洲進口 48.2 億美元，增 9.5 億美元(+24.6%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 10 月自主要國家/地區進口與上年同期比較，自東協增 85.6%、
自南韓增 72.7%、自中國大陸與香港增 45.3%、自日本增 27.4%、自
美國增 23.0%、自歐洲增 12.1%。

六、主要國家/地區貿易概況 
（一）今年 1-9 月主要國家/地區出口均呈上升，其中南韓(1-10 月)、香港、
新加坡受 AI 相關需求帶動科技供應鏈及區域貿易活動熱絡，累計出
口分別年增 60.6%、52.3%、41.3%；中國大陸、美國各年增 21.3%
及 14.3%，德國(1-8 月)、日本各增 8.8%、8.3%。 
（二）主要國家/地區進口同步成長，累計 1-9 月以香港進口年增 51.8%較
高，新加坡、中國大陸、南 韓 (1-10 月)分別年增 39.0%、27.5%及 21.4%，
德國(1-8 月)、日本、美國各增 8.4%、8.2%、6.7%。

### Page 7

7
圖 5 2026 年主要國家/地區出進口年增率
七、綜合分析
（一）隨全球人工智慧(AI)基礎建設投資強勁成長，帶動相關供應鏈需求擴
增，加以外銷產品價格攀升，10 月出口達 967.6 億美元，首度突破
900 億美元，年增 58.9%，連續 36 個月正成長；進口 688.9 億美元，
同為歷年單月最高，年增 75.1%，主要受惠於 AI 產業之國際分工與
出口引申需求增加，以及國際原物料行情高漲；出超 278.8 億美元亦
創新高。累計 1 至 10 月出口年增 47.7%、進口增 44.6%。
（二）10 月出口主要貨品中，資通與視聽產品、電子零組件出口值雙創單月
新高，各年增 72.9%及 61.4%，主因 AI、高效能運算及雲端服務等應
用商機熱絡；其餘貨類出口平均增 25.7%，其中礦產品因油價支撐增
71.6%，機械、電機產品受惠於半導體業者擴充產能及 AI 需求外溢，
增幅皆達 3 成，基本金屬及其製品亦增 35.7%。累 計 1 至 10 月資通與
視聽產品、電子零組件合占總出口比重約 8 成，年增 60.6%。

（三）10 月對主要市場出口年增率均逾 4 成，以對東協增 83.8%較突出，
對美國、日本、歐洲出口規模皆為單月新高，各增 55.9%、49.6%及
46.0%，對中國大陸與香港增 42.9%。累 計 1 至 10 月對五大市場出口
增幅介於 28%~59%，其中對美國出口年增 58.8%、對東協增 56.1%，
占總出口比重各為 31.7%及 20.1%，分別寫下近 36 年同期及歷年同
期新高紀錄。
（四）展望未來，全球經濟仍面臨地緣政治衝突、美國貿易政策走向等不
確定因素，惟隨人工智慧應用逐漸擴散至推論、代理式 AI，且國際
雲端服務業者持續布建 AI 基礎設施，帶動半導體、伺服器等相關供
應鏈需求延續，加以科技新品相繼推 出、年底消費旺季來臨之挹注，
我國第 4 季出口可望維持強勁成長態勢。

1476.TT 儒鴻 
 評價低緣、高殖利率，投資價值浮現

[法說會更新]   
   公司簡介 
儒鴻成立於 1977 年，生產各式針織布及成衣，從染整、織布、定型、成衣，擁有垂
直整合生產供應鏈，並切入機能性服飾市場，2025 年針織布營收 127.7 億元，YoY-
2.3%，營收占比 33.61%，成衣營收 252.2 億元，YoY+6.2%，比重占 66.39%。 
投資評等/目標價 重點摘要 
買進 416 
【前次投資建議】買進，396 
【大盤指數/股價】42712 / 348.5

➢ 3Q26 獲利略低於投研部預估 
公司 3Q26 營收 105.72 億元，YoY+9.7%，營收略低於投研部預估之
106.8 億元，主要係因成衣營收低於投研部預期所致，毛利率 31.18%，
優於投研部預期，主要受惠高毛利率新產品及新客戶占比提升，且匯率
亦有所助益(約影響毛利率 0.62 個百分點)，費用率因業績獎金提列較高
而推升至 10.49%，營益率 20.69%，低於投研部預估之 21.24%，稅後
EPS 6.36 元，低於投研部預估之 6.57 元。 
➢ 4Q26 展望正向，略為上修 4Q26 獲利預估 
10/2026 營收 34 億元，YoY+10.7%，符合投研部預期，本次法說會管
理層表示對 4Q26 營運維持 TITAS 2026 釋出之看法不變，4Q26 營收可
望為年內高峰，上修 4Q26 營收預估至 105.99 億元，YoY+14.4%，毛
利率因原料成本負面影響趨緩，公司高毛利率新產品及新客戶占比持續
提升，毛利率同步上修至 31.05%，YoY+0.86ppt.，預估稅後淨利 17.25
億元，YoY+9%，稅後 EPS 上修至 6.29 元。目前訂單能見度維持 6 個
月，展望正向，期中選舉民主黨勝選預期對關稅壁壘政策可望有所趨緩，
且美伊戰爭亦可望有所緩解，市場不確定因素減少亦有利於整體經濟消
費動能，印尼廠一期、二期目前維持穩定獲利且產能滿載，投研部上修
2026 年營收預估至 408.77 億元，YoY+7.6%，毛利率因新產品、新客
戶營收占比持續提升，略為上修至 31.09%，YoY+2.45ppt.，稅後 EPS
上修至 24.99 元。  
➢ 2027 年營運成長可期，評價處歷史區間低緣且殖利率逾 5%  
公司目前始承接 04/2027 訂單，觀察美國總體經濟動能強勁，消費者支
出可望維持韌性，惟考量公司現有廠區產能趨於滿載，目前僅以原有產
能小規模擴充為主，投研部以過去幾年公司中性成長均值預估 2027 年
營收 431.95 億元，YoY+5.7%，毛利率因 4Q27 印尼新廠投產而略為承
壓，預估 2027 年毛利率 30.62%，YoY-0.47ppt.，營益率預估 20.74%，
YoY+0.01ppt.，稅後 EPS 預估由 26.19 元略為上修至 26.31 元，投研部
以現金股利配發率 74.63%預估，現金股利殖利率可望達 5.35%，且依
未來四季合計稅後 EPS 估算目前評價仍處過往歷史區間低緣位置，長期
投資價值浮現，投研部維持評價 PER 16X 並以未來四季稅後 EPS 估算目
標價，目標價由 396 元上修至 416 元(4Q26~3Q27F PE 16X)，維持買
進投資建議。

儒鴻為國內成衣廠龍頭 
儒鴻成立於 1977 年，生產各式針織布及成衣，從染整、織布、定型、成衣，擁有垂
直整合生產供應鏈，並切入機能性服飾市場，2025 年針織布營收 127.7 億元，YoY-
2.3%，營收占比 33.61%，成衣營收 252.2 億元，YoY+6.2%，比重占 66.39%；營收
區域別來看，2025 年美洲營收占比 50.87%，YoY+4.5%，亞洲營收占比 35.19%，
YoY+2.6%，歐洲營收占比 10.29%，YoY+10.2%，中東營收占比 2.49%，YoY-17.5%，
紐澳營收占比 0.85%。YoY+16.2%，非洲營收占比 0.31%。YoY-64.7%。 
3Q26 獲利略低於投研部預估  
公司 3Q26 營收 105.72 億元，QoQ+4.8%，YoY+9.7%，營收略低於投研部預估之
106.8 億元，主要係因成衣營收低於投研部預期所致，針織布營收則優於投研部預期，
毛利率 31.18%，QoQ+1.17ppt.，YoY+2.52ppt.，優於投研部預估之 30.76%，主要
受惠高毛利率新產品及新客戶占比提升，且匯率亦有所助益(約影響毛利率 0.62 個百
分點)，費用率因業績獎金提列較高而推升至 10.49%，高於投研部預估之 9.53%，營
益率 20.69%，QoQ+1.51ppt.，YoY+1.59ppt.，低於投研部預估之 21.24%，業外則
有匯兌收入認列 1,327 萬元，稅後淨利 17.44 億元，QoQ+12.3%，YoY+5.6%，稅
後 EPS 6.36 元，低於投研部預估之 6.57 元。

個股報告 
4Q26 展望正向，略為上修 4Q26 獲利預估 
10/2026 營收 34 億元，MoM-3.1%，YoY+10.7%，YTD+5.9%，占投研部原預估
4Q26 營收達成率 33.4%，符合投研部預期，公司過去 10 月單月營收占 4Q26 營收
占比區間介於 29.5%~34.2%，本次法說會管理層表示對 4Q26 營運維持台北紡織展
(TITAS 2026)釋出之看法不變，以訂單來看，若出貨船期順利，則 4Q26 營收可望為
年內高 峰， 基此 ，投研 部 上修 4Q26 營收 預估 至 105.99 億 元，QoQ+0.3%，
YoY+14.4%，毛利率因原料成本負面影響趨緩，公司高毛利率新產品及新客戶占比持
續提升，毛利率亦同步上修至 31.05%，QoQ-0.14ppt.，YoY+0.86ppt.，並同步上修
營益率至 20.33%，QoQ-0.36ppt.，YoY+1.17ppt.，預估稅後淨利 17.25 億元，QoQ-
1.1%，YoY+9%，稅後 EPS 由 5.84 元略為上修至 6.29 元。 
展望 2026 年，訂單能見度維持 2 個季度，訂單展望維持正向，公司表示目前訂單尚
未因美國期中選舉結果出爐後而有所變動，然期中選舉民主黨勝選預期對關稅壁壘政
策可望有所趨緩，且美伊戰爭亦可望有所緩解，市場不確定因素減少亦有利於整體經
濟消費動能，印尼廠一期、二期目前維持穩定獲利且產能滿載，目前協力廠外發比重
維持 27%~28%，因 4Q26 營收及獲利略為上修，投研部亦同步上修 2026 年營收預
估至 408.77 億元，YoY+7.6%，毛利率部份，因高毛利率新產品、新客戶營收占比可
望 持 續 提 升 ， 且 匯 率 環 境 相 較 於 2025 年 亦 較 為 友 善 ， 略 為 上 修 31.09% ，
YoY+2.45ppt.，營益率則由 20.62%上修至 20.73%，YoY+2.06ppt.，並預估稅後淨
利 68.56 億元，YoY+24.3%，稅後 EPS 由 24.77 元上修至 24.99 元。 
圖 1：公司歷年月營收趨勢               單位：千元

資料來源：國票投顧彙製 
2027 年營運可望維持成長，目前評價處歷史區間低緣且殖利率逾 5% 
展望 2027 年，目前已開始承接 04/2027 訂單，雖訂單能見度未達 2027 年底，且客
戶端 2027 年預示單仍洽談中，然觀察美國就業環境仍穩健，初領及續領失業救濟金
人數仍處歷史低檔，總體經濟動能強勁，消費者支出可望維持韌性，且 2026 年營運
受戰爭、通膨影響較為劇烈，研判 2027 年可望較為淡化，惟考量公司現有廠區產能
趨於滿載，印尼三期實際貢獻營收時點落於 4Q27~1Q28，目前僅以原有產能小規模
擴充產能為主，若市況優於預期則以外發協力廠因應，2027 年外發比重大致上介於
27%~35%區間，基此，投研部以過去幾年公司中性成長均值預估 2027 年營收 431.95
億元，YoY+5.7%，主要為產品報價提升之貢獻，並預估毛利率因 4Q27 印尼新廠投

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
企業活動維持擴張，消費信心與房市疲弱，通膨及關稅風險仍存 
經濟 
⚫ 10 月美國綜合 PMI 產出指數初值由 58.4 降至 57.6，企業活動仍強勁擴
張，內需及出口帶動訂單與就業成長；惟油價、關稅及供應鏈瓶頸推升
成本，售價漲幅創 2022 年 8 月以來新高，通膨壓力持續升溫，後續須
關注聯準會政策動向。 
⚫ 9 月美國住宅市場續顯疲弱，建築許可及新屋開工分別年減 3.0%及
7.4%，反映住宅投資動能放緩。儘管多戶住宅許可與完工數回升，但整
體營建活動仍偏弱，後續仍須關注房貸利率、住房負擔能力及營建成本
對市場復甦的影響。 
⚫ 密西根大學公布之 10 月美國消費者信心指數降至 48.0，月減 0.2%、年
減 10.4%，顯示民眾對目前經濟環境仍缺乏信心，雖然對未來景氣的看
法略有改善，但高物價與借貸成本仍讓消費者感到壓力。此外，未來一
年及長期通膨預期分別升至 4.7%及 3.5%，反映民眾仍擔心物價持續上
漲。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 10 月 23 日川普宣布調整加拿大商品 50%關稅清單，10 月 30 日生效，
豁免部分紙製品、機械及包裝材料，並新增地毯、油漆等課稅項目。外
媒認為此舉與期中選舉有關，有助降低部分企業成本，但美加貿易摩擦
仍未化解。 
短評 
⚫ 10 月美國綜合產出指數雖略為下滑，但仍維持高檔，顯示內需與就業持
續支撐企業活動擴張。然而，消費者信心仍處低檔，反映高物價與借貸
成本持續壓抑購買意願，儘管民眾對未來景氣的看法略有改善，但新屋
開工及建築許可同步下滑，顯示消費與住宅投資前景仍面臨挑戰。另一
方面，美國調整部分加拿大商品關稅，雖有助減輕部分企業進口成本，
但雙邊貿易摩擦尚未化解，政策不確定性仍存。

歐 
元 
區 
法國動盪短期內難降溫，料將使央行保持審慎觀望態度 
經濟 ⚫ 歐元區 10 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)下降 1.3 點至 51.9，服務業、製 造業 PMI 分別降 1.3、0.5 點至 51.7、52.9。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 歐洲議會代表團於 10 月 27 日至 29 日訪問中國，料將討論稀土及綠能
技術的依賴、貿易失衡，以及歐洲企業在中國面臨的不平等待遇問題。 
⚫ 法國抗爭活動規模逐漸擴大，各大工會與抗議團體已宣布在 10 月 31 日
再度發起大規模遊行，並預告於 11 月 5 日舉行全國性大罷工。 
短評 
⚫ 法國政府既無法強力通過重大的財政改革，也難以快速推出安撫反對派
的妥協法案或者公共支出。部分較為悲觀的機構認為此波動盪可能會延
續至 2027 年 4 月的法國總統大選。 
⚫ 市場預期歐洲央行在 10 月 28 日的政策會議將採取觀望立場。 
日 
本 
貿易逆差及通膨壓力延續，內需仍具韌性 
經濟 
⚫ 9 月出口年增 16.7%。 
⚫ 9 月全國消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 2.1%。 
⚫ 10 月綜合 PMI 持平於 52.3。 
短評 
⚫ 出口維持強勁、服務需求亦持續擴張，惟中東局勢推升能源進口成本，
使貿易逆差擴大，並加重國內通膨壓力。隨成本上漲逐步由商品擴散至
服務價格，通膨黏著性可能進一步提高，為日本央行貨幣政策正常化提
供支撐；另一方面，原物料供應受限及投入成本居高不下，已開始影響
製造業動能與企業信心，後續仍須關注高成本環境對經濟成長的制約。 
中 
國 
第三季經濟成長率回升，但前景仍較為不利 
經濟 
⚫ 2026 年第三季 GDP 年增率初估值為 4.5%。 
⚫ 1-9 月累計零售額年增率持平為 1.1%。 
⚫ 1-9 月固定資產投資年減幅維持於 7.2% 
⚫ 1-9 月房地產開發投資額年減幅由 19.9%擴大至 20.6%。 
⚫ 9 月工業生產年增率較 8 月增加 0.3 個百分點至 5.5%；累計 1-9 月工業
生產年增率持平 1-8 月水準為 5.3%。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 歐盟計劃最快將於 2027 年首季擴大對中國產品的進口限制，涵蓋化學
品、家具及機械等產品，不再僅限電動車。 
⚫ 中國銀行成功發行市場首筆掛鉤 DR001 基準利率的「三農」專項金融

債券，被視為近年中國央行把貨幣政策重心自逆回購利率(政策利率)，

⚫ 儘管政府 10 月初宣布釋放 5,500 億元人民幣的地方政府債務結存限額，

特定高科技產業及扶植內需來減輕貿易環境惡化的衝擊。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.10.27 
一、 美國—企業活動維持擴張，消費信心與房市疲弱，通膨及關稅風險仍存 
標普全球(S&P Global)公布，2026 年 10 月美國綜合 PMI 產出指數(Composite 
PMI Output Index)初值由上月的 58.4 降至 57.6，其中服務業採購經理人指數
(Services PMI)由 58.8 降至 57.9，製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI)則由
55.9 微升至 56.0，創 2022 年 5 月以來新高，企業活動雖略有放緩，但仍維持強
勁擴張。需求方面，內需穩健及出口改善帶動整體新訂單增速創逾 4 年半新高，
商品出口訂單亦為 2025 年 6 月以來首次增加；企業持續擴增人力，整體就業增
速為 2022 年 6 月以來次高。另一方面，未完成訂單增加、供應商交貨延遲加劇，
加上油價上漲及關稅推升能源、運輸與原物料成本，使企業投入成本維持高檔，
帶動售價漲幅升至 2022 年 8 月以來最高。儘管企業對未來一年營運前景的樂觀
程度略降，但仍處於今年相對高檔。整體而言，美國經濟在內外需及就業支撐下
維持擴張，惟供應鏈瓶頸與成本上升加劇通膨壓力，後續仍須關注物價走勢及聯
準會貨幣政策動向。 
密西根大學公布，2026 年 10 月美國消費者信心指數終值降至 48.0，略低於
上月的 48.1，月減 0.2%、年減 10.4%，並較今年 2 月下降約 15%，顯示消費者信
心持續處於低檔。分項來看，當前經濟狀況指數由 50.9 降至 46.7，月減 8.3%、
年減 20.3%；消費者預期指數則由 46.3 回升至 48.8，月增 5.4%、年減 3.0%，反
映民眾對未來景氣的看法略有改善，但整體信心仍偏弱。調查指出，高利率及購
買負擔壓力使耐久財購買條件下降 18%，拖累當前經濟狀況指數；未來一年企業
景氣預期雖改善約 14%，但仍低於去年同期 16%。通膨預期方面，未來一年通膨
預期由 4.6%升至 4.7%，長期通膨預期亦由 3.4%升至 3.5%，雙雙連續第二個月攀
升，創今年 5 月以來最高水準。密西根大學指出，2025 年關稅政策衝擊及 2026
年伊朗衝突引發的能源價格上漲，使民眾通膨預期再度升溫，且長期預期已明顯
高於 2019 年及 2024 年水準。整體而言，10 月美國消費者信心大致持平，但高物
價與借貸成本仍壓抑購買意願，加上短期及長期通膨預期持續上升，可能增加物
價回穩的難度，後續仍須關注能源價格、家庭消費及通膨預期變化對聯準會貨幣
政策的影響。

### Page 6

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

5

2026 年 9 月美國住宅市場建築許可年化總數降至 140.0 萬戶，月減 0.2%、年
減 3.0%，其中單戶住宅建築許可降至 84.5 萬戶，月減 4.0%、年減 4.8%，惟 5 戶
以上多戶住宅建築許可增至 50.3 萬戶，月增 5.9%、年增 1.0%，顯示不同住宅類
型開發規劃表現分歧。新屋開工年化數降至 122.1 萬戶，月減 5.2%、年減 7.4%，
其中單戶住宅開工 85.9 萬戶，較去年同期仍增 2.6%，但多戶住宅開工降至 34.9
萬戶，年減 25.9%，拖累整體營建動能。施工方面，在建住宅維持 128.0 萬戶，與
上月持平但較去年減少 3.3%；完工年化數則回升至 119.2 萬戶，月增 2.2%，主要
受多戶住宅完工月增 14.9%帶動，惟整體仍較去年同期減少 14.0%。整體而言，
美國住宅市場呈現建築許可及新屋開工下滑、在建規模持平及完工略有回升的態
勢，反映住宅營建活動仍偏弱，後續仍須關注房貸利率走勢、住房負擔能力及營
建成本變化對住宅市場復甦的影響。 
美國總統川普 10 月 23 日依據《1930 年關稅法》第 338 條簽署公告，調整部
分加拿大商品加徵 50%關稅的適用範圍，並自 10 月 30 日起生效。此次調整取消
膠合板、部分紙製品、機械與冷凍設備、包裝用木箱及紙箱、新鮮與乾燥海藻等
商品的額外關稅，同時新增閥門、地毯、油漆、洗髮精、可可粉、木製棺材、空
調設備及獨木舟等課稅項目，呈現局部鬆綁與新增課稅並行的政策方向。白宮表
示，此舉旨在減輕加拿大貿易措施對美國企業造成的負擔，並維護國內產業利益。
由於緬因州農業及製造業高度依賴加拿大進口物資，共和黨參議員柯林斯(Susan 
Collins)積極向白宮爭取關稅豁免，外媒亦將此次調整與 11 月 3 日期中選舉連結。
整體而言，新措施有助降低部分美國企業的進口成本，但美加貿易摩擦尚未完全
化解，後續仍須關注雙邊談判及關稅政策對供應鏈的影響。 
二、歐元區—法國動盪短期內難降溫，料將使央行保持審慎觀望態度 
 歐元區 10 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)下降 1.3 點至 51.9，抵銷上月的強勁
增幅，但為連續五個月高於 50 的榮枯線，主要係受到法國大規模抗議活動的影
響，使法國綜合 PMI 下降 2.7 點至 48.4；此外，德國綜合 PMI 下降 1 點至 52.8，
亦反映近期通膨壓力增加，使民眾傾向保守消費。若以產業區分，歐元區服務業、
製造業 PMI 分別降 1.3、0.5 點至 51.7、52.9；惟德國製造業 PMI 上升至相當強勁
的 54.2，反映德國政府擴張軍備投資以及基礎建設之需求，持續推動製造業生產
動能。

6

法國抗爭活動規模逐漸擴大，各大工會與抗議團體已宣布在 10 月 31 日再度
發起大規模遊行，並預告於 11 月 5 日舉行全國性大罷工，抗議主要訴求包括改
善公共服務品質及凍結物價上漲。然而，在財政壓力巨大且國會缺乏穩定執政多
數的情況下，法國政府既無法強力通過重大的財政改革，也難以快速推出安撫反
對派的妥協法案或者公共支出。故部分較為悲觀的機構認為此波動盪可能會延續
至 2027 年 4 月的法國總統大選。受法國局勢影響，近期歐元區各國殖利率普遍
呈現上升趨勢，並對景氣產生暫時性的抑制效果。在此背景下，市場預期歐洲央
行在 10 月 28 日的政策會議將採取觀望立場，後續關注歐洲央行是否將透過政策
訊號或流動性工具，為平息法國主權債務利率而採取行動。 
三、日本—貿易逆差及通膨壓力延續，內需仍具韌性 
 9 月出口雖維持強勁，受半導體製造設備及汽車出口增加所帶動，出口額年增
16.7%；惟進口增幅更為顯著，9 月進口額年增 18.9%，其中原油及液化天然氣進
口額分別大增 51.1%及 39.2%，主要反映中東局勢推升能源價格，以及日本尋求
替代能源供應來源的影響。由於進口額高於出口額，9 月貿易逆差達 5,365 億日
圓，為連續第 5 個月呈現逆差；累計 2026 上半財年（4 至 9 月）貿易逆差亦較上
年同期擴大 98.8%至 2.82 兆日圓。整體而言，出口並未明顯轉弱，近期貿易逆差
擴大主要反映能源進口成本攀升，使進口增幅持續高於出口。 
 9 月全國消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 2.1%，即使政府透過能源及教育補貼等措
施抑制物價，通膨仍高於 2%，顯示實際通膨動能更為強勁。從物價結構來看，石
油、原物料及工資成本上升已逐步傳導至終端價格，漲價範圍由家具、醫療用品
及休閒用品等商品，進一步擴散至房屋維護、公共運輸、住宿及外食等服務項目，
顯示企業成本轉嫁範圍逐步擴大，通膨壓力亦由商品向服務領域擴散。隨通膨持
續高於 2%且漲價範圍擴大，亦為日本央行進一步推動貨幣政策正常化提供支撐。 
 10 月綜合 PMI 持平於 52.3，顯示經濟仍維持穩定擴張，其中製造業 PMI 下
降 0.8 點至 53.3，產出指數亦降至 3 月以來最低，主要反映新訂單及出口訂單成
長放緩，加上稀土金屬等原料供應受限，對生產形成一定壓力，惟生產與訂單仍
處擴張區間，尚未顯示製造業景氣明顯轉弱；服務業 PMI 回升 0.7 點至 52.0，新
業務及出口訂單同步改善，顯示服務需求仍具韌性，為支撐景氣的重要動能。另
一方面，能源、薪資及原物料成本居高不下，使投入價格壓力持續偏高，並逐漸

影響企業對未來景氣的信心。 
四、中國—第三季經濟成長率回升，但前景仍較為不利 
2026 年第三季 GDP 年增率初估值為 4.5%，較第二季的 4.3%上揚 0.2 個百分
點；累計 1-9 月 GDP 年增率則為 4.6%。第三季的二、三級產業年增率分別較第
二季上揚 0.4、0.1 個百分點至 3.4%，其中製造業、批發零售業年增率分別達 5.9%、
2.3%為主要動能，抵銷建築業年減 4.4%等拖累因素影響。整體而言，第三季 GDP
年增率上揚有部分受到中秋國慶連假落在 9 月末段(去年同期則於 10 月 1 日開
始)，致基期效果發揮部分影響，相關消費動能仍可能會在 10 月出現暫時性的回
落。 
在需求指標方面，(1)1-9 月累計零售額年增率為 1.1%，持平於 1-8 月的累計
年增率。其中 9 月零售額年增率增加 0.7 個百分點至 1.1%，部分受到新手機上市
帶動買氣，9 月通訊器材消費年增率高達 31.1%，加上中秋國慶長假帶動食品消
費年增率增加 1 個百分點至 2.1%所致；(2)1-9 月固定資產投資年減幅維持於 7.2%，
反映供應鏈移轉、政府主導去化過剩產能等因素導致民間投資需求急遽萎縮；萎
縮產業包含化工、醫藥、專用設備、汽車製造業、金屬製品業等；(3)1-9 月房地
產開發投資額年減幅由 19.9%擴大至 20.6%、施工面積年減幅為 12.8%，新建商
品房銷售面積年減幅擴大為 13.2%，房市投資、買氣皆持續低迷。 
受到出口維持高度成長帶動，9 月工業生產年增率較 8 月增加 0.3 個百分點
至 5.5%(累計 1-9 月工業生產年增率持平 1-8 月水準為 5.3%)。依產業別來看，9
月主要受到煤礦開採、化工、化纖等產業擴張影響，主因中秋與國慶長假前發電
業充實庫存水位，促使煤炭企業加快復產，加上油價上漲帶動相關中下游供應鏈
增產；惟高科技產品包含 IC、機器人、新能源車等年增率皆有放慢，反映去年中
秋國慶連假皆落在 10 月，使得去年 9 月工作日較多，致基期相對墊高的影響。 
展望未來，儘管政府 10 月初宣布釋放 5,500 億元人民幣的地方政府債務結存
限額，但額度下達到資金撥付往往需要數月時間，預期對於第四季的經濟影響有
限，使今年全年經濟成長率預估將落在 4.5%附近。此外，受歐盟右派勢力崛起及
保護主義影響，歐盟對中國貿易政策可能持續趨嚴，後續仍須關注雙方談判及溝
通結果，未來若外需動能減弱，預期中國政府將持續透過發展特定高科技產業及
扶植內需來支持經濟成長動能，以減輕房市不振及貿易環境惡化的衝擊。

五、印度及東南亞—新、馬出口保持強勁增速，菲律賓央行大舉升息 
(1)印度: 10 月 HSBC 綜合採購經理人指數（PMI）初值上升 0.8 點至 56.7，其中，
製造業 PMI 略下滑 0.2 點至 54.9、服務業 PMI 則上揚 1.0 點至 56.2，皆保持穩健
擴張態勢。由於美國、英國、中東及孟加拉等地的訂單增加，帶動新訂單及出口
訂單加速成長，製造商採購庫存增速創八個月來最快、成品庫存增速亦創歷史新
高，然而聘僱指數降至今年以來最低、未完成訂單下降，反映現階段人力充足。
另一方面，投入成本漲幅連續第七個月下降並降至近一年低點，產品售價漲幅亦
明顯放緩，通膨壓力緩解，有助提升企業獲利能力及消費者購買力，並且給予印
度央行（RBI）貨幣政策空間。 
(2)新加坡：9 月非石油國內出口（NODX）年增 68.4%，增速史上最快，連續第
23 個月正成長，其中，人工智慧需求強勁，帶動電子產品出口躍增 107.6%；非電
子產品出口亦大幅成長 54.2%，主要由船舶和遊艇組件所帶動。按出口目的地劃
分，對香港出口激增 121.1%、對台灣、韓國出口增幅達 96%，對美出口成長 73.2%，
但對印度出口下降 5.6%。展望未來，新加坡金融管理局在 10 月的宏觀經濟評估
報告中表示，直至 2027 年人工智慧 (AI) 驅動的成長將保持相對穩定，支持未
來幾季經濟成長表現。由於基數較高以及人工智慧相關投資預計放緩，全球利率
上升可能會對新加坡主要貿易夥伴的消費和非人工智慧投資造成壓力，可能會影
響外部需求，薪資成長放緩、招聘謹慎以及運輸成本上升可能會在 2026 年下半
年對可自由支配支出和零售活動構成壓力，預計 2027 年 GDP 成長將放緩。 
(3)印尼：印尼央行（BI）10 月會議連續第四次會議維持基準利率(7 天期逆回購利
率,BI-Rate)於 5.75%不變，貸款便利利率（LFR）維持在 6.50%不變，但將隔夜存
款便利利率（DFR）調高 25 個基點至 5%，旨在提高印尼盾資產的回報率，藉此
吸引並留住外資，同時宣布分兩階段調降存款準備率，11 月 1 日起下調 1 個百分
點至 8%，12 月 1 日起進一步降至 7%，以提供銀行體系流動性。整體而言，印尼
央行爲捍衛印尼盾，在 5 至 6 月間共升息 100 個基點，同時調降外資避險成本，
並採取「資金停留愈久、優惠愈高」的機制吸引外資流入。惟近期國際指數編纂
公司 MSCI 擬將印尼股市由”新興市場”降級為”獨立市場”，引發資金外流疑慮，
央行選擇利率按兵不動，搭配降準提供銀行體系流動性，以兼顧印尼盾穩定與國
內經濟需求，未來 MSCI 檢討進展(最快 2027 年 4 月公布)及印尼盾走勢，將持續
牽動央行後續政策。

(4)菲律賓：菲律賓央行（BSP）10 月 22 日宣布大幅調升基準利率(逆回購利率)2
碼至 5.50%，為 2023 年以來最大幅度升息。央行指出， 9 月通膨率已升至 7.2%，
遠高於 2%-4%的目標區間；同時核心通膨持續攀升，顯示價格上漲已由食品與能
源擴散至整體經濟。BSP 亦擔憂中東情勢推升國際油價、運輸費率調漲，以及聖
嬰現象衝擊農業生產，進一步推高物價。此外，披索貶至歷史低點附近，增加進
口通膨風險，因此採取較激進的緊縮措施以穩定市場預期，貨幣政策重點已由支
持經濟成長轉向優先遏制通膨風險。 
(5)馬來西亞：9 月出口額年增 54.5%至 MYR2,151 億元，其中電子&電氣產品出
口大增 69.2%，持續反映 AI 帶動的強勁需求，礦產品出口亦大幅成長 56.3%，抵
銷農產品出口續跌 5.7%的影響，對台灣、美國出口大增 175.2%、89.9%、對新加
坡出口增幅亦達 50.4%。9 月進口額成長 52.7%至 MYR1,814 億元，受惠出口引
申需求，中間產品、資本貨物進口分別成長 69.6%、20.9%，消費品進口轉為正成
長 18.2%，自中國和新加坡進口分別成長 67.6%、90.7%。進出口相抵，9 月貿易
順差達 MYR337 億元、年增 64.9%。 
六、台灣—外銷訂單創新高，產業景氣冷熱不均 
9 月外銷訂單達 1,142.1 億美元，再創單月新高，年增 62.4%，主要受惠於人
工智慧、高效能運算與雲端服務等應用蓬勃發展，以及消費性電子新品陸續推出，
帶動相關供應鏈維持高檔；加上國際原物料價格上漲，推升整體接單表現。惟各
產業接單動能仍呈分化，科技產品需求強勁，傳統產業景氣則相對疲弱。經濟部
指出，雖有年底節慶補貨潮挹注，但傳統產業部分產能過剩，終端需求仍未見明
顯回升。

——2026年 10月物价数据点评
[Table_Industry]日期: shzqdatemark [Table_Summary] 主要观点
价格走势分化，PPI 涨幅扩大
本月 CPI 同比环比均有所回落，食品价格是主要的下降项，而上涨项
仍然是汽油为主的能源价格。汽油价格同比上涨 22.4%，涨幅比上月
扩大 5.8个百分点，影响 CPI同比上涨约 0.63个百分点，带动能源价
格涨幅由上月的 7.3%扩大至本月的 9.9%。扣除食品和能源价格的核
心 CPI 同比上涨 1.0%，小幅回升，基本保持稳定。 PPI 同比上涨
5.7%，涨幅比上月扩大 0.7个百分点。价格上涨的主要行业有，煤炭
开采、石油开采、石油加工、化学制品、燃气、计算机通信、有色金
属矿采选、有色金属冶炼、电气机械，上述 9个行业合计影响 PPI 同
比上涨约 6.14个百分点，上拉影响比上月增加 0.64个百分点。

1 事件：10月 CPI、PPI数据公布
国家统计局数据显示，2026年 10月份，全国居民消费价格同
比上涨 0.7%。其中，城市上涨 0.7%，农村上涨 0.7%；食品价格
下降 2.9%，非食品价格上涨 1.5%；消费品价格上涨 0.8%，服务
价格上涨 0.7%。1—10月平均，全国居民消费价格比上年同期上
涨 0.9%。10月份，全国居民消费价格环比持平。其中，城市持平，
农村下降 0.1%；食品价格下降 1.0%，非食品价格上涨 0.2%；消
费品价格下降 0.1%，服务价格上涨 0.1%。
2026年 10月份，全国工业生产者出厂价格同比上涨 5.7%，
环比上涨 0.7%。工业生产者购进价格同比上涨 7.9%，环比上涨
1.1%。1—10月平均，工业生产者出厂价格比上年同期上涨 2.7%，
工业生产者购进价格上涨 4.0%。
表 1：10月 CPI、PPI同比数据（%）
资料来源：IFIND，上海证券研究所
2 事件解析：数据特征和变动原因

2.1 CPI小幅回落
本月全国 CPI环比由上月上涨 0.2%转为持平。食品价格由上
月上涨 0.4%转为下降 1.0%，影响 CPI环比下降约 0.17个百分点，
是 CPI 环比下降的主要原因。其中，猪肉、鲜菜、鸡蛋、鲜果、
水产品价格均有不同程度的下降。非食品方面，受国际市场价格
变动影响，国内汽油价格上涨 4.2%，涨幅比上月回落 0.7 个百分
点，影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.14个百分点；服务方面，服务价格由
上月下降 0.2%转为上涨 0.1%，影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.04个百分
点，其中受国庆长假的刺激，相关旅游出行等服务价格有所上涨。
CPI 同比上涨 0.7%，涨幅比上月回落 0.2 个百分点。具体来
看，食品价格下降 2.9%，降幅比上月扩大 1.2 个百分点，影响
CPI 同比下降约 0.49 个百分点。食品中，鲜菜、猪肉和鲜果价格
分别下降 16.9%、8.6%和 3.8%，合计影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.54
个百分点。非食品方面，受汽油涨价影响，能源价格涨幅扩大。

汽油价格上涨 22.4%，涨幅比上月扩大 5.8 个百分点，影响 CPI
实际值 上月值 去年同期
CPI同比 0.7 0.9 0.2
PPI同比 5.7 5.0 -2.1

至本月的 9.9%。扣除能源的工业消费品价格上涨 1.2%，涨幅比

涨。服务价格上涨 0.7%，涨幅比上月回落 0.1 个百分点，影响
CPI同比上涨约 0.34个百分点。

降 8.6%；蛋类价格上涨 10.1%，影响 CPI上涨约 0.05个百分点

本月 CPI 同比环比均有所回落，食品价格是主要的下降项，
而上涨项仍然是汽油为主的能源价格。汽油价格同比上涨 22.4%，
涨幅比上月扩大 5.8 个百分点，影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.63 个百分
点，带动能源价格涨幅由上月的 7.3%扩大至本月的 9.9%。扣除
食品和能源价格的核心 CPI 同比上涨 0.8%，涨幅比上月回落 0.2
个百分点，基本保持稳定。PPI 同比上涨 5.7%，涨幅比上月扩大
0.7个百分点。价格上涨的主要行业有，煤炭开采、石油开采、石
油加工、化学制品、燃气、计算机通信、有色金属矿采选、有色
金属冶炼、电气机械，上述 9 个行业合计影响 PPI 同比上涨约
6.14个百分点，上拉影响比上月增加 0.64个百分点。
4 事件预测：趋势判断
4.1 推动经济向新向优向好发展
10月价格持续分化，CPI走势偏稳，PPI持续上涨。CPI同比
的变动越来越受到非食品的影响，尤其是汽油价格，但由于食品
项偏弱的表现，CPI 整体平稳，核心 CPI 也保持稳定。工业品价

格同比涨幅有所扩大，上游原材料与下游制造业均有所涨价，尤
其是人工智能持续赋能产业转型带动计算机通信、电气机械等行
业上涨突出。政策方面，7月政治局会议提出要稳定生猪等农畜产
品生产和价格，实施好更加积极的财政政策和适度宽松的货币政
策，充分发挥各项存量政策效能，及时谋划出台务实管用的增量
政策，加大逆周期调节力度，推动经济持续向新向优向好发展，
努力实现“十五五”良好开局。
5 风险提示：
地缘政治事件恶化，国际金融形势改变，中美政策超预期变
化。

Economic Indicators

2026 年 11 月10 日

1/2 
2026 年 7-9 月期 GDP 予測(最終版) 
～前期比年率▲0.4％の小幅マイナス成長を予想～ 
経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト 新家義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

11 月16 日に公表される26 年７-９月期の実質
ＧＤＰ成長率を前期比年率▲0.4％（前期比▲
0.1％）と予測する。10 月 30 日の段階では前期
比年率＋0.4％（前期比＋0.1％）と小幅プラス
成長を予想していたが、その後公表された経済
指標の結果を反映し、小幅マイナス成長へと予
測値を下方修正する。 
本日公表された26 年９月分の国際収支統計の
結果を反映し、26 年７-９月期の実質輸出を前期
比＋0.1％（従来予測値：同＋1.2％）へ下方修
正する。筆者の９月分の想定と比べてサービス
輸出が大きく下振れたことが主因である。実質
輸入も前期比＋3.0％（従来予測値：同＋
3.3％）と下方修正するが、輸出の下方修正度合
いの方が大きいことから、外需の予測値を前期
比年率寄与度▲1.9％Pt（従来予測値：同▲
1.3％Pt）へ下方修正している。 
また、９月分の家計調査と家計消費状況調査
が想定よりも下振れたことを反映し、個人消費
の予測値も前期比▲0.3％と、従来予測値の同▲
0.2％から小幅下方修正した。

物価高による下押しや耐久財需要の反動に加え、天候不順の影響もあって個人消費が減少する
ことが成長率を押し下げる見込みである。特に、８～９月には長雨・大雨や台風などの天候不順
が相次ぎ、外出手控えや旅行・宿泊の見送りなどを通じて消費が下押しされたとみられる。一
方、設備投資は高い伸びとなり、景気を下支えするとみられる。設備投資の伸びには特殊要因も
（％）

2/2
一部含まれているが、それを除いても堅調だったと考えられる。
７-９月期は４四半期ぶりのマイナス成長になると予想するが、マイナス幅は大きくない。今回
のマイナス成長には天候不順という一時的な下押し要因も含まれており、過去３四半期に高い成
長が続いた後であることや、企業部門の底堅さが維持されていることを踏まえれば、景気回復の
流れが途切れたと判断する必要はないだろう。
物価高による節約志向は根強く、個人消費の回復にはなお時間を要するとみられるが、設備投
資を中心に企業部門が好調さを維持している点は好材料である。家計部門が力強さを欠く一方、
企業部門が景気を下支えする構図のもと、日本経済は均してみれば緩やかな回復基調にあると判
断している。

崩れず～ | 新家 義貴 | 第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所（10 月 30 日発行）をご参照ください）

1 
2382 廣達電腦(Quanta)

報告日期：2026/11/12 研究員：曾智楷(Kevin Tseng) 
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 3Q26 營收 1 兆 1155 億，YoY +125%，毛利率 4.75%，稅後
淨利 284 億，稅後 EPS 7.08 元。 
 4Q26 新世代 GPU 平台 AI 機櫃預計將進入小批量量產，通用
型伺服器出貨量亦將持續成長，而筆電第四季出貨量則預計將
呈雙位數季減。 
 公司預估 2027 年，伺服器佔總營收比重將進一步攀升至
85%~90%，其中 AI 伺服器佔比將突破 80%。筆電則受記憶體
價格影響，預估營收佔比將降至個位數百分比。

一. 3Q26 毛利率 4.75%，EPS 7.08 元 
廣達 3Q26 營收 1 兆 1155 億，QoQ +7.6% ，YoY +125.2% ，產
品組合部分，受惠於 AI 機櫃 ASP 持續走高，第三季 AI 伺服器營
收呈現季增，但出貨量則較上季略減，主要由於第三季處於新舊
產品世代的轉換期。通用伺服器受惠於 AI Agent 與資料中心需求
擴大，第三季通用型伺服器出貨量表現優於預期。而筆電部分，
第三季出貨量為 960 萬台，QoQ -17%，YoY -24%，主要由於記
憶體漲價影響，以至於今年下半年終端市場需求明顯疲弱。獲利
方面，3Q26 營業毛利 530.2 億，QoQ +1.8%，YoY +56.3%，毛
利率 4.75%，QoQ -0.27ppts，YoY -2.1ppts，公司表示，毛利率
下滑主因高單價 AI 機櫃出貨 ASP 大幅躍升產生稀釋效應，加
上筆電材料成本上揚與規模縮減所致，並非本業獲利能力惡化。
營業利益 322 億，QoQ -6.0% ，YoY +75.3% ，營利率 2.89%，
QoQ -0.41ppts ，YoY -0.82ppts 。3Q26 業外淨利 26.8 億，稅後
284.2 億，QoQ -0.8%，YoY +73.0%，單季稅後 EPS 7.08 元。

二. 4Q26 AI 伺服器動能延續，筆電持續面臨逆風 
公司公告 10 月營收 3532.5 億，MoM +8.6%，YoY +104%，今年
累計營收 3.31 兆，YoY +99.9%。展望 4Q26，公司表示，隨著新
世代 GPU 平台 AI 機櫃於第四季逐步進入小批量量產，受惠於新
產品 ASP 顯著拉升，第四季 AI 伺服器營收仍將維持強勁季增。
通用型伺服器第四季出貨量亦預計持續成長，而筆電第四季出貨
量則預期呈雙位數季減。另外，毛利率部分，由於高單價 AI 機櫃
出貨佔比提升持續稀釋毛利率，再加上筆電出貨規模縮減，預期
4Q26 毛利率將較第三季下滑。預估 4Q26 營收 1.15 兆，QoQ 
+3.8%，YoY +81.3%，毛 利率 4.7%，營利率 2.9%，稅後淨利 290

三. 預估 2026/2027 年 EPS 26.75/31.86 元 
展望 2026 年，公司預估，2026 全年伺服器佔比將達 80%~85%。
其中 AI 伺服器表現優於原先倍增目標，佔整體伺服器營收比重約
75%~80%，佔今年全年營收約 60%~65%。通用型伺服器亦優於
預期，呈雙位數年增，而筆電部分，則受到終端需求疲弱影響，
全年出貨量預計年減 15%~20%。預估公司 2026 全年營收 4.12
兆，YoY +94% ，毛利率 4.81%，營利率 2.99%，稅後淨利 1073
億，全年稅後 EPS 26.75 元。展望 2027 年，隨著新世代 AI 機櫃
於 1Q27 放量，ASP 提升約 50%至接近翻倍，AMD 專案亦將持續
出貨，再加上 ASIC 業務預計明年將有雙位數的成長，預估 2027
年 AI 伺服器營收將維持高雙位數年增；通用型伺服器 2027 年出
貨量與營收亦將維持雙位數增長。預估 2027 年伺服器佔總營收比
重將進一步攀升至 85%~90%，其中 AI 伺服器佔比將突破 80%。
而筆電則受記憶體價格居高不下影響，預估 2027 年出貨量將年減
高個位數至 10%，營收佔比將降至個位數百分比。資本支出方面，
今年前三季資本支出為 346 億元，公司先前宣布終止友達華亞廠
收購案，將轉向擴大海外佈局，預估 2027 年資本支出將略增至
400~450 億元，主要投入美國加州與泰國廠區，以支應未來 12~24
個月 CSP 客戶明確的產能預留需求。預估公司 2027 全年營收 5.85
兆，YoY +42% ，毛利率 4.37%，營利率 2.79%，稅後淨利 1278
億，全年稅後 EPS 31.86 元。

四. 投資建議為「買進」，目標價 385 元 
受惠於 AI 伺服器的強勁需求，公司預估明年 AI 伺服器營收將雙
位數成長，且隨美系四大 CSP 資本支出展望維持正向，GPU、ASIC
伺服器專案持續放量，將有望挹注公司營運持續增長。以公司 2027
年 EPS 來看，目前股價交易於 9~10 倍本益比，評價仍具上修空
間，給予買進投資建議，目標價為 385 元(12x 2027 EPS)。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
廣達電腦(Quanta)為全球筆記型電腦專業研發設計製造與雲端運
算解決方案領導供應商，為全球前五大筆記型電腦代工廠商。公
司主要替品牌業者從事代工服務，代工項目包含筆記型電腦、平
板電腦、智慧型手機、伺服器、商用及家用智慧物聯網裝置、消
費性電子產品、車用電子等。公司總部位於桃園龜山，生產基地
包含桃園、大陸上海、重慶、江蘇、美國田納西州、加州、墨西

圖一、廣達製造基地及全球據點

資料來源：廣達

二.產業趨勢概況 
1. 明年 CSP Capex 預估破 1.5 兆美元，AI 伺服器產值年增逾 9 成 
根據集邦科技(TrendForce)最新研究，受惠於 Google、Amazon、
Meta、Microsoft、Oracle 及中國四大科技巨頭等全球前九大雲端
服務供應商(CSP)擴大投資 AI 基礎設施，預估 2026 年九大 CSP
資本支出總額將逾 9,570 億美元，YoY +106%，2027 年更因伺服
器整機漲價、零組件缺貨漲價及供應商要求預付款等因素，有望
突破 1.5 兆美元，YoY +60%，雙雙優於預期。在超大型 CSP、Tier-2
資料中心與主權 AI 等需求帶動下，預估 2026 年 AI 伺服器產值約
4,790 億美元，占整體伺服器產值近 75%；2027 年隨輝達 GB300、
VR200 與超微 Helios 等整櫃式方案成為主流，加上記憶體等關鍵
零組件短缺推升單價，預計整體伺服器產值將首度跨越 1 兆美元，
其中 AI 伺服器產值將躍升至近 9,200 億美元 YoY +90%，占比進
一步提高至 81%左右。

圖二、2024-2027 年九大 CSP 資本支出總額預估(單位:十億美元)

資料來源：TrendForce、宏遠投顧整理

2. 2026 年全球 AI 伺服器出貨量預計年增 31% 
根據 TrendForce 最新 AI server 產業研究，近期超大型 CSP、
Tier-2 資料中心業者對 NVIDIA GB/VR 等整櫃式 AI server 方案的
採購意願明顯提高，且 Google、AWS 新一代 ASIC 平台於 2026
下半年陸續放量，加上中系業者擴大採用本地 AI 方案以滿足雲端
大型語言模型(LLM) AI 需求，促使 TrendForce 上調 2026 年 AI 
server 出貨年成長幅度，從原先的 28%更新為近 31%。北美五大
業者 2026 年的資本支出多翻倍提升，反映出大型 CSP 廠持續擴
建 AI 資料中心、GPU 叢集、液冷散熱等新一代基礎設施，以應對
生成式 AI 及大型模型快速成長帶來的算力需求。其中，Google 
2026 至 2027 年都將側重自家 TPU 晶片發展，2027 年出貨量有
望繼續高速成長。AWS 2026 年以 NVIDIA GB300 作為 GPU AI 
server 主力，自研 ASIC 也穩定出貨，預期至 2027 年量能將再擴
大。Meta 2026 年主要採用 NVIDIA GB/VR、AMD Helios 整櫃式
方案，自研 ASIC 布局預計至 2027 年將明顯加速，為該公司長期
降低 AI 基礎設施成本、提升推論效率的重要策略。

### Page 1

• Anticipating another strong year into 2027 — MPI has maintained its 
current guidance for capacity plan into next year: year end-2026 capacity 
targets of VPC 2m and MEMS 3.5m, and 2027 MEMS expansion is expected 
to be at least another 2.5m. However, we see potential upside thanks to 
robust customer demand and market share gain. CPO insertion 2 is under 
final evaluation and insertion 3 already starts engineering  shipments  to 
more than 5 international customers across foundry, OSAT and fabless 
segments. The company sees strong growth momentum of probe card into 
next year and CPO equipment contribution at single digit in 2027 with more 
upside into 2028.
• We stay constructive on MPI despite recent market concerns regarding AI 
demand slowing down. The key drivers are continuing to ramp-up at 
existing AI ASIC customers, incremental contributions from new AI/non-AI 
ASIC and CPU customers, further MEMS capacity expansion, and potential 
upside from CPO and microbump probe-card technology.

### Page 2

MPI (6223.TWO)

2
MPI
Valuation
Our target price for MPI is set at NT$7,200, based on a target PE multiple of 52x applied to our 2027-28E EPS average. We
believe our PE target, at the high end of MPI's 3-year forward PE range, is justified given MPI’s earnings power remains
structurally higher than that in the previous cycles, broadening demand across AI ASIC, additional CPU opportunities and non-
AI ASIC wins. We expect MPI to see accelerating earnings growth ahead supported by sustainable sales growth and an

improving mix. At our target price, the shares would trade at 2027E/28E P/B of 23x/15x.
Risks
Our quant risk rating system rates the MPI stock as High Risk for its share-price volatility. Key downside risks that could
prevent MPI shares from reaching our target price include 1) slowdown in AI ASIC demand; 2) slower-than-expected adoption
of MEMS probe cards; 3) intensifying competition from global and regional peers; 4) slow development of the CPO equipment

business.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do

Date: 2026-11-12

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We review Hon Hai’s 3Q26 results, announced 12 November. 
We are impressed with its opex control, which led to solid operating margin 
expansion despite gross margin dilution from rising AI server contribution.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results beat. Net income was TWD78bn (EPS: 
TWD5.53), 25% higher than our estimate (TWD62bn) and 10% higher than 
the Bloomberg consensus (TWD70bn). Revenue was TWD3.0tn (+19% 
QoQ; +46% YoY) with better-than-expected smartphone contribution. The 
gross margin came in at 6.02% (vs. 6.12% in 2Q26), a 0.1pp decline QoQ 
on a rising cloud segment mix. That said, the operating margin expanded 
0.07pp to 3.83%, driven by strong opex control and better leverage.

Key updates: Hon Hai reaffirms its positive view of its 2027 revenue 
momentum, targeting double-digit growth YoY (similar to our forecast). The 
company believes its cloud business will remain a key growth driver (54% 
of total revenue in 3Q26). Regarding total AI server racks, Hon Hai expects 
to see high-double-digit growth in 2027 (vs. our expectation of c.30% YoY 
growth from GPU server plus a rising ASIC contribution; however, as ASIC 
AI servers typically utilise consignment models, its AI server revenue 
growth will be slightly diluted). As for smartphones, considering Hon Hai’s 
key clients’ models command high ASPs, we believe the impact from rising 
memory costs will be relatively smaller, but we expect the PC segment to 
decline YoY due to the negative impact from rising component costs.

During the 3Q26 results call, AI server end demand from the US was the 
hottest question, especially after the US mid-term elections. We believe 
management’s view on hyperscalers’ and neocloud’s near-term demand 
remains positive, but the long-term impact must be closely monitored. Also, 
as investors are worried about the AI server capex slowdown from CSPs, 
management also indicated that there would be new end demand from 
sovereign and enterprise groups, which could act as a second growth 
wave. For 4Q26, the company is guiding for >15% revenue growth QoQ 
and YoY given the ICT peak season along with rising AI server revenue (up 
by 5-15% QoQ due to ongoing AI server shipment strength).

What we recommend: We raise our 2026-28E EPS by 10-12% to reflect 
higher ASP assumptions for AI servers. We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating but 
trim the 12M TP to TWD320, using a lower target PER of 13x (prior 16x; vs. 
its past-3-year range of 8-18x) on our 1-year forward EPS, given the overall 
weaker sentiment. Downside risk: low demand for AI servers.

How we differ: Our 2026-27E revenue are above the consensus by 2-3%, 
likely as we are more optimistic on Hon Hai’s AI server shipments.

12 November 2026

3Q26 review: solid opex control

➢ OPM expansion on strong scale leverage despite GM contraction  
➢ Demand outlook remains promising even after US mid-term elections 
➢ Reiterating Buy (1) rating; trimming 12M TP to TWD320

Company profile 
Established in 1974, Hon Hai Precision Industry (Hon Hai) started out making plastic products, 
connector products and cable assemblies. It is now the largest electronics manufacturing service (EMS) 
provider globally in terms of design, manufacturing, global logistics and after-market service.

### Page 5

5

Hon Hai Precision Industry (2317 TT): 12 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 12 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Hon Hai has incorporated 4 independent members into its 7-member board of directors in pursuit
of better governance. However, the company's Chairman also serves concurrently as President,
implying an absence of separation between board leadership and executive management.
Capital management 1 Hon Hai has declared >40% payout ratio for most of the years since 2014, and >50% in the past 5 years, pointing to a shareholder-friendly dividend policy.
Related party &
transaction 2
Revenue from related parties accounted for 1.6% of total revenue for 2025, while purchases
from related parties constituted 0.1% of cost of sales. We believe there is limited risk from
related-party transactions.
S Supply chain management 2 Hon Hai adheres to green design concepts and incorporates environmental factors and pollution prevention measures during the design and development stages. The company's green product design guidelines include 4 axes: "energy-saving, non-harmful, reduction and easily recyclable."
S Labor practices 2
Hon Hai recognises employees as the most important asset and continues to invest in its
workforce. In terms of safety, Hon Hai has upgraded its occupational safety and health
management system since 2019, and obtained the ISO45001 and CNS45001 certificates. The
company held online activities related to factory safety and health. Overall, we note that Hon Hai
has improved in terms of labour management in recent years. Hon Hai established a team to
implement data security management. Moreover, it plans to conduct data security training and
education regularly for all employees. Hon Hai aims to achieve zero data security incident in the
future.
S Product design & lifecycle management 2 Hon Hai adheres to green design concepts and incorporates environmental factors and pollution prevention measures during the design and development stages. The company's green product design guidelines include 4 axes: "energy-saving, non-harmful, reduction and easily recyclable."
Company specific Management Analyst comments
E GHG emissions 2
In November 2020, Hon Hai announced its commitment to achieving net zero greenhouse gas
emissions by 2050. Its Scope 1 and 2 GHG emissions decreased by 53.6% in 2025 compared
with 2020, exceeding its reduction target of 21%. That said, the company's actual energy-saving
rate in mainland China was 5.2% in 2025, successfully achieving its energy-saving goal of 4.2%.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

3Q26 Review: 4 분기 실적 반등 기대감 Up  
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 600,000 원(유지) 
 현재주가 292,500 원(10/29)

 동사의 3분기 실적은 매출액 45.1조원(-3.4%yoy), 영업이익 2.68조원
(+5.5%yoy, OPM 5.9%), 지배이익 1.7조원(-24.6%yoy)을 기록하며 시장 
컨센서스를 상회. 도매 판매 감소, 원가 부담 증가 영향 있었으나 판매보증
충당금 축소와 원가 절감 활동을 통해 수익성 방어 
 실적 증감 요인: 영업이익 증감 요인은 물량 -1.11조원, 믹스/인센티브 -
0.57조원, 금융 -0.11조원, 환율 +1.28조원, 기타 +0.64조원 등. 원자재 
가격 상승으로 5천억원의 비용 증가 요인 있었으나 위기 대응 원가 절감 
활동으로 대부분 상쇄할 수 있었음. 전분기 대비 기말환율 급락함에 따라 
판매보증충당금 지난 해 1.26조원에서 340억원으로 약 1.23조원 감소. 판
보충 효과는 일회성에 그치겠지만 원가 절감 효과 컸던 점 긍정적  
 금융 부문: 금융부문 매출액은 전년비 2.8% 증가했지만 영업이익은 26% 
급감. 장기 금리 상승에 따른 이자 비용 증가와 기업금융 충당금 적립, 중고
차 관련 비용 확대가 수익성 악화의 주요 원인. 중고차 관련 비용은 EV 리
스 반납 차량의 매각 손실 확대에 기인한 것 
 4분기 전망: 동사는 국내 공장 정상화와 아반떼, 투싼, GV90, GV80 HEV 
등 주요 신차 효과를 통해 4분기 생산 확대와 주요 수출 시장 공급 회복이 
가능할 것으로 전망하였음. 원가 부담은 지속되나 물량 감소에 따른 영향에
서는 벗어날 수 있을 것. 원자재 가격 상승에 대응해 원가절감 프로그램 확
대와 신차 출시를 통한 인센티브 정상화를 통해 수익성 개선 지속할 계획 
 연간 가이던스 하향 조정: 글로벌 도매 판매 목표를 기존 416만대에서 
400만대로 하향 조정. 매출 성장률은 +1~2%, OPM 가이던스는 기존 
6.3~7.3%에서 5.5~6.5%로 하향 조정. 자동차 부문 투자 계획은 17.8조
원에서 16.0조원으로 축소. 핵심 R&D 투자는 유지하되 Capex 집행 시기 
및 우선 순위 조정할 계획 
 4분기 실적 반등 전망: 4분기 주요 신차(투싼, 아반떼, GV80 HEV, GV90) 
출시 본격화로 3분기까지의 부진을 만회할 수 있을 것으로 기대. 원가 부담 
은 비용 절감 활동을 통해 충분히 만회가 가능한 것으로 3분기 실적을 통
해 확인됨. 본격적인 주가 반등을 위해서는 판매 회복 이상의 주가 트리거 
필요할 것으로 보이나, 4분기 반전의 실마리가 나타나고 있다는 점에 주목. 
동사에 대한 투자의견 매수와 목표주가 60만원을 유지함

시가총액(십억원) 59,514

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: November 16-20

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD:
November 16-20
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 14 Nov 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Monica Sauro / Unsplash

### Page 3

Last week’s inflation data ran hot. October’s headline CPI rose 0.4% m/m,
while the core CPI rose 0.3% for a third straight month. The PPI for final
demand rose 0.3% m/m in October after a revised 1.4% in September,
lifting the y/y rate to 6.5%, the highest since November 2022.
This week’s calendar is busy, with the FOMC minutes, Nvidia’s earnings,
Anthropic’s IPO, retail sales, and industrial production. Here’s more:
(1) FOMC minutes & FedSpeak. Minutes of the October 27-28 FOMC

meeting (Wed) should show how many of the 19 participants (12 voters
and 7 nonvoters) leaned toward a rate hike. The Committee held the
federal funds rate (FFR) target range at 3.75%-4.00% in a 10-2 vote, with
Fed Governor Lisa Cook and Cleveland Fed President Beth Hammack
dissenting in favor of a 25bps increase.
Six Fed officials speak this week (Warsh, Williams, Musalem, Hammack,
Goolsbee, and Paulson). Fed Chair Kevin Warsh appears Monday at the

Fed’s AI and productivity task force hearing in the San Francisco Bay
Area. New York Fed President John Williams, who speaks Monday and
Wednesday, said on Thursday that “inflation is far too high” and that
further tightening will be needed. We think the Fed should and will hike by
50bps in December.
FFR futures imply 3.6 rate hikes of 25 bps over the next 12 months
including 2.6 over the next six months (chart). The odds of a 50bps hike at
the December meeting are just 11.5% currently.

### Page 4

The 2-year Treasury bond yield is at 4.86%, well above the 4.00% top of
the FFR target range, with 12-month futures at 4.78% (chart).
(2) Nvidia earnings & Anthropic IPO. Nvidia reports Q3 earnings on
Tuesday, its first report since Jensen Huang’s resignation as CEO.
Revenue is expected to be $109.1 billion, up from $57.0 billion a year
earlier and slightly above the company’s $108 billion guidance. The
stock’s forward P/E is down to 11.5 (chart). The conference call with

interim CEO Colette Kress, who remains CFO, is likely to be more
revealing than the numbers.

### Page 5

Anthropic shares are expected to begin trading Thursday, with a price
range of $70-$78. That implies a fully diluted valuation of $1.26 trillion to
$1.40 trillion. We feel confident about the company’s prospects based on
its S-1 filing.
(3) Retail sales. October retail sales (Tue) follow September’s 0.8% m/m
gain, with sales ex autos up 0.9%. Retail sales ex food services rose 6.2%
y/y in September, and Redbook same-store sales continue to run well

ahead of the official measure (chart).
(4) Manufacturing. November’s NY and Philly Fed surveys (Mon, Thu)
follow solid October readings. Their average edged up to 23.2 in October
from 22.7 in September. The average has run well ahead of
manufacturing production growth this year, which was just 1.4% y/y in

### Page 6

September (chart). Historically, the two have tracked more closely,
suggesting that factory output could accelerate in the months ahead.
October’s industrial production (Tue) follows September’s 0.7% m/m gain,
which lifted the index to 104.0. Capacity utilization was 76.9%.
Manufacturing aggregate weekly hours slipped to 84.0 in October from
84.2 in September, suggesting that factory output weakened last month
(chart).
(5) ADP & unemployment claims. ADP’s weekly employment reading

(Tue) will update a four-week average that has been rising for the past two
weeks (chart).

### Page 7

Initial jobless claims (Thu) follow last week’s 200,000, up from 189,000,
with the four-week average at 195,250 (chart). Continuing claims fell to
1,697,000 for the week ended October 30, with the four-week average
steady at 1,709,000.

Document 341: Europe’s Deal Limiting Chinese Auto Imports (2026-11-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Europe’s Deal Limiting Chinese Auto Imports (2026-11-02).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 16
Status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
Europe’s Deal Limiting Chinese Auto Imports
Europe’s auto deal with China is a good start. But containing China’s export boom
will require a much broader response.
European Commissioner for Trade and Economic Security Maros Sefcovic speaks at a press briefing at the EU Delegation to
China in Beijing, China October 9, 2026. Florence Lo/Reuters
PUBLISHED
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
November 2, 2026 11:02 a.m.
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
The year-over-year increase in Chinese automobile
exports to all markets is currently about 3.5 million
cars.
Over ten years, that is 35 million cars. The global
market excluding China is only around 60 million
cars. Japan raised its auto exports by about 3.5 million
cars in the 1970s—but that was over a decade, not
one year. And right now the U.S. and Indian markets

(around 20 million cars) are effectively closed to
Chinese production. The 2026 jump in exports is an
incredible.
China’s domestic market, though, is down 20 percent
this year—that works out to a decline of about 5
million cars. The Wall Street Journal estimates that
domestic sales will fall to around 19 million cars this
year, less than a third of the production capacity of
China’s auto industry.
So even though China’s exports are increasing at an

unprecedented pace, China’s domestic auto sector
isn’t really booming; production is essentially flat, and
intense domestic competition is destroying the
profitability of firms that don’t export.*

### Page 3

3
Europe’s new auto deal with China should be
understood in this context.
The deal limits China’s exports of hybrids (including
plug-in hybrids) to Europe to around 350,000 cars.
That is above the 300,000 hybrids that China
exported to Europe in 2025, but well below the (gulp)
800,000 annualized pace for hybrids in the last three
months of (European) data. Total imports,
annualized, were running at an incredible 2 million a
year pace thanks to strong battery electric imports in

September.

### Page 4

4
China appears to have concluded that it prefers a
guaranteed market for a limited number of cars (and
more or less guaranteed profits) to the risk of a
prohibitive tariff that would cut off all its exports.
And Europe preferred a negotiated deal to the risk of
retaliation and a slowdown in licensing rare earth and
magnet exports.
The Commission also thinks of this as a bit of a
model—more on that later.
I certainly understand the logic of the deal.

Something needed to be done. China’s export of
hybrids to Europe were up an insane 200 percent
year-over-year in the last 3 months of data, which led
to a 75 percent increase for all cars. One more year of
75 percent growth would bring China’s exports to

### Page 5

5
around 3 million cars; in two years, close to 6 million,
and in three years, 10 million—or most of the market.
But Europe still is a long way from a coherent trade
and industrial policy with respect to autos, let alone a
coherent economic policy towards overall trade with
China.
After this deal, as I understand it:
Hybrid imports are capped at 350,000, down
from a projected 800,000 in 2026—and then
rising to 370,000 and 390,000 in subsequent
years.

Battery electric vehicles face a tariff from 25
percent (Telsa, 10 percent base + 15 percent) to
45 percent (10 percent base plus 35 percent).
BYD’s total tariff is at 27 percent, which isn’t
prohibitive. Even with these tariffs, battery
electric imports will total around 600,000 this
year, up from 500,000 last year.
Diesel and gasoline (ICE) cars face a tariff of
10 percent. Europe is importing around
200,000 of these a year (including a significant

number from Chinese-owned MG).
There are no special tariffs on imported parts or
imported batteries, which matters for the growing
number of Chinese factories in Europe. So long as
tariffs are low and the renminbi is undervalued,
Chinese firms still have an incentive to maximize the

### Page 6

6
Chinese content of their “transplant” factories (ask
Thailand).
There is currently no unified European policy on
inward investment that requires joint ventures or
technology transfer either, only a lot of wishful
thinking.**
Sum it up, and it is a market where Chinese exports
are constrained by a hodge-podge of different trade
defense tools, and there is no standard policy for
ensuring that Chinese assembly operations use
European parts rather than import Chinese kits. The

existing operations, which are just starting to scale up,
don’t draw on a European supply chain. They likely
preserve a few assembly jobs at the expense of the
broader automotive ecosystem.
A coherent European (or national) industrial policy
for the automotive and battery sectors is thus still a
work in progress.
The safeguard on hybrids and the resulting tariff rate
quota only stops the bleeding.
And make no mistake, Europe’s position in the global

automotive sector is slipping. Europe still runs an
overall surplus in auto trade, but that surplus is falling
fast if trade in batteries is added to automotive trade
(there isn’t a split between batteries used for energy

### Page 7

7
storage and batteries used by cars in the EU data
right now, at least not that I have found).
Europe’s broader trade policy to China is similarly a
work in progress.
The surge in imports of hybrid cars was particularly
pronounced, but it is by no means the only such surge.
The balance in industrial machinery has eroded
significantly. Consider the following chart, which
shows imports and exports of machinery, electronics
and vehicles (HS 84, 85, and 87) by weight.

### Page 8

8
The balance in chemicals, measured by weight to
abstract from price changes, shows the same trend.
Europe doesn’t have an across-the-board tool for
applying tariffs against China like the U.S. section
301 statute.

### Page 9

9
But that isn’t necessarily an immediate constraint, as
there are plenty of solid safeguard cases thanks to the
current surge in European imports from China.
But if each safeguard needs to include a negotiated
tariff-rate quota that gives China enough guaranteed
market access (and Chinese firms enough guaranteed
profits) to ensure trade peace, Europe needs to get
moving with negotiation in other sectors.
Chemicals and machinery are more diverse sectors

than cars and thus aren’t as amenable to the same
high-level Š ef č ovi č -Wang negotiations the led to the
most recent deal.
This critique isn’t meant as criticism. Europe needed
to act on cars. Look at a chart of automotive trade in
kilos.

### Page 10

10
It’s just that it also needs to act in other sectors: auto
parts, batteries (though that is complicated),
chemicals, and some categories of industrial
machinery. The scale of China’s export boom and the
fact that in most sectors there are no limits to China’s
ability to scale up is a real problem.***
And Europe cannot wait for China to conclude that
it would rather grow on the basis of internal, rather
than external, demand.
I disagree with the IMF’s former top brass on this. A

China that grows on the back of a 1.5 pp net export
contribution for another couple of years will
deindustrialize much of the world. China’s trading
partners cannot step aside and do nothing until
China figures out how to escape deflation using its
own demand. Xi appears more than happy to
continue with his current policy mix and gamble that

### Page 11

11
the world will continue to absorb China’s ever
growing export surplus.
Pascal Lamy, Nicolas Köhler-Suzuki, and Victor Luiz
do Prado of the Jacques Delors Institute recognize
the need for a more systemic response—as do Jean-
Pierre Landau and Sébastien Jean, who have done an
excellent job explaining how China’s micro- and
macro-industrial policies fit together into a coherent,
self-reinforcing whole.
The proposals in the Lamy et al. paper overlap heavily

with the Commission’s current approach to China.
The authors envision a two-pronged approach: first,
negotiating with China to seek agreement on tariff-
rate quotas that effectively limit the EU’s imports
from China; and second, developing a broad yet
targeted tariff-based instrument to address structural
imbalances should negotiations fail. The latter would
give Europe a credible threat should China refuse to
agree to the proposed safeguards.
My own view: don’t let the perfect be the enemy of

the good.
The EU has shied away from developing its own 301
tool out of concern about preserving a “rules-based
system.” I still think a European 301 would allow a
more fulsome response, but understand that
European isn’t as willing as the United States to use

### Page 12

12
tools that at least appear to be outside those
authorized by the WTO. In reality the EU no longer
really wants to trade with China on the same (MFN)
terms applied to other WTO members. That mirrors
the current view of the United States. But the EU is
willing to go through more procedural hoops to get to
a similar result.****
But I also recognize that, absent policy adjustment in
China—notably a stronger currency that allows for
fair market competition—the scope of safeguards and

managed trade will progressively expand.
* One sign: Stellantis is resuming Jeep production in
China, but primarily for export rather than the
domestic market.
** There is a lot of (in my view) somewhat confused
analysis of Chinese investment that works off the
analogy provided by either foreign investment in
China’s auto sector or Japanese investment in the U.S.
and Europe in the 1980s. These are relevant examples
of course, but asserting that Chinese investment will

result in a similar outcome when the underlying
economics differ is a big logical jump. Japanese firms
had incentives to localize their parts production in the
United States from the then-strong yen as well as
from the risk of protection. Foreign firms operating in
China had to do JVs until recently and had incentives
to localize from China’s significant tariff on auto

### Page 13

13
parts and China’s lower cost structure (in part because
of a weak RMB). Tesla’s Shanghai incentives and tax
deal included strong localization incentives, and there
were additional incentives from Tesla’s desire to
qualify for the lists the governed access to China’s
consumer EV subsidies. That is why something like
90 percent of Tesla parts, including its batteries, are
now made in China. Chinese investors in Europe
have no similar incentives to localize right now, yet

many assume that the results will be the same. That,
to me, is naïve. Since the underlying economics differ
(Europe’s cost basis is much higher than existing
production in China) getting Chinese firms to do
more than final assembly in Europe will take stronger
policy tools than were required to get foreign firms to
localize parts production in China. Europe’s
Industrial Accelerator Act would be a step in the
right direction, but more is likely needed. Thailand

provides a useful cautionary example.
*** China can make over 60 million cars, according to
recent reporting from the Wall Street Journal. The
domestic market this year will be under 20 million
cars (down from something like 24 million cars last
year). Even with exports of 10 million, there is ~30
million cars worth of unused auto production
capacity (roughly double European demand). And
China is still investing at a pace that is consistent
with adding close to 5 million of EV production

capacity a year. Chinese firms, who don’t make any
money on domestic sales, are strongly incentivized to

### Page 14

14
This work represents the views solely of the author(s). The
Council on Foreign Relations is an independent,
nonpartisan membership organization, think tank, and
publisher, and takes no institutional positions on matters
of policy.
From Follow the Money and Greenberg Center for Geoeconomics
export. The world has never encountered such elastic
supply.
**** The more different the legal basis for the
European and U.S. response—and the more policy
differences (TRQs vs. a flat tariff, for example) the

harder it would be to ever develop a harmonized
policy approach. But with President Trump, a
harmonized policy isn’t even a U.S. goal.

Document 395: NZ labour market review chartpack, Q3 2026 (2026-11-18)

Source PDF: all report pdf/NZ labour market review chartpack, Q3 2026 (2026-11-18).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-18
Pages: 14
Status: ok

### Page 1

LABOUR MARKET CHARTPACK
4 Nov 2026

### Page 2

Westpac New Zealand Economics
2
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5670 | michael.gordon@westpac.co.nz      Connect with us
Key points – September quarter 2026
• The September quarter surveys pointed to ongoing softness in labour market conditions. The unemployment rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing an
almost five-year run of increases. The unemployment rate is now at its highest level since September 2015.
• There were mixed messages in terms of jobs growth: the Household Labour Force Survey reported a 0.1% fall in employment, while the employer-

focused Quarterly Employment Survey suggested a modest rise. The Monthly Employment Indicator had also pointed to a small rise in jobs.
• The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in the participation rate. These co-movements essentially reversed the surprisingly large
increases that we saw in the June quarter, which we had suggested at the time were more likely due to sampling error in the survey rather than a
genuine surge.
• We generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate, which is less vulnerable to sampling errors – and the message here is one of softness. Our

best interpretation is that, to the extent that jobs are growing, it’s not fast enough to absorb the growth in the population.
• While the economy has returned to growth over the last year or so, hiring activity remains weak. This may be an ongoing legacy of employment trends in
previous years, when firms were relatively reluctant to lay people off during the slowdown, which then potentially left them overstaffed even as activity
started to pick up again. It’s not clear how long this effect can persist, though.

• Wage growth remains muted. Although cost-of-living pressures have increased on workers, the existing degree of spare capacity in the labour market
doesn’t give them much leverage to negotiate larger pay increases.
• Overall, we judge these results to be weaker than the RBNZ expected, in particular the rise in the unemployment rate. This data supports the views of
those on the policy committee who were less convinced in October about the potential for second-round inflation pressures in the economy.

### Page 3

Westpac New Zealand Economics
3
Employment
• The number of people employed fell by 0.1% in the September quarter, according to the
Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS). Previous quarters were revised down a little,
reflecting downward revisions to the working-age population estimates. Annual employment
growth slowed from 0.9% to 0.7%.
• This was substantially softer than our forecast of a 0.5% rise for the quarter. Our forecast
was based on the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI), a comprehensive measure that is

drawn from income tax data. While there are some differences in coverage for these two
measures, any significant divergence between the two is most likely to reflect sampling error
in the HLFS.
• Meanwhile, the employer-focused Quarterly Employment Survey (QES) recorded a 0.1% rise
in the number of filled jobs and a 0.6% rise in hours paid. (This survey is of course vulnerable
to sampling error as well.)
• We’re most inclined towards the MEI measure of employment, given the breadth of its

coverage. But our best interpretation is that, to the extent that jobs are growing, it’s not
yet fast enough to absorb the growth in the adult population – hence the further rise in the
unemployment rate.

Michael Gordon, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5670 | michael.gordon@westpac.co.nz      Connect with us

### Page 4

Westpac New Zealand Economics
4
Labour force participation
• The surprising fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a drop in the participation rate
from 70.6% to 70.4%.
• These co-movements are effectively a reversal of the surprisingly large increases that we
saw in the June quarter, which we noted at the time were suggestive of sampling error in the
survey. Any such errors largely wash out when it comes to the unemployment rate, however,
which is why we recommend focusing on this measure.

• Setting aside the usual quarterly volatility, labour force participation remains caught
between two competing forces. One is cyclical weakness, especially among younger people,
who are finding it difficult to break into a “low-fire, low-hire” labour market. The teen
unemployment rate of around 25% is a record high going back to the start of the survey
in 1986.
• The other force is rising participation among the over-60s (who also make up a growing

share of the population). Kiwis have had a growing propensity to work beyond the NZ Super
age, as there is no penalty for doing so. It’s unclear to what extent this can be considered
an increase in the pool of available workers: older workers tend to have low mobility
between jobs, and if they find themselves out of work are more likely to leave the labour
force altogether.

Michael Gordon, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5670 | michael.gordon@westpac.co.nz      Connect with us

### Page 5

Westpac New Zealand Economics
5
Unemployment and underutilisation
• The unemployment rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7% in the September quarter. This was the
highest rate since September 2015.
• While there are other indicators of spare capacity in the labour market, we’re not convinced
that there is additional information to draw from them this time.
• The broader underutilisation measure, which includes discouraged jobseekers and part-
time workers wanting more hours, was steady at 13.8% after having risen sharply from

12.9% last quarter. This measure is both higher (by definition) and more variable than the
unemployment rate, but tells a similar story in that it’s around an 11-year high.
• There was a marked 1.6% fall in the number of hours worked (although ‘usual’ hours were
down just 0.4%). However, we suspect this was largely driven by Matariki – a floating holiday
that won’t be captured in the seasonal adjustment.
• This may also account for the 2.5% rise in part-time employment, against a 0.7% fall in

full-time employment. Some workers may have been pushed into the part-time category
(less than 30 hours a week) simply by virtue of only working four days during the
survey week.

Michael Gordon, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5670 | michael.gordon@westpac.co.nz      Connect with us

### Page 6

Westpac New Zealand Economics
6
Hiring
• Business confidence surveys have shown a lift in hiring intentions in recent months, as firms
have shaken off the initial shock of the Iran conflict and the surge in fuel prices.
• However, those stated intentions aren’t obviously turning into reality yet. Job advertisements
were down about 1% for the September quarter in seasonally adjusted terms, and they
remain well below pre-Covid levels. Job ads will capture ‘churn’ within existing roles as well

as new job creation, but both of these would be associated with a strong labour market.
• With the working-age population growing at the same time, the result was a modest fall in
the vacancy rate, one of the RBNZ’s highest-ranked measures of labour market tightness.
• We estimate that there are around 1.9 people unemployed for every job vacancy, up from
around 1.8 people this time last year.
• Firms have told us that they were reluctant to lay off workers during the economic downturn

in 2023-24, as they were wary of the extreme labour shortages that had developed in the
previous boom years. As a result, they may find themselves still overstaffed even as demand
has started to pick up again, and haven’t needed to return to hiring yet.

### Page 7

Westpac New Zealand Economics
7
Population and migration
• The working-age population grew by around 1.0% over the last year, compared to a peak of
3.3% growth over 2023. Jobs growth (based on the less volatile MEI measure) is still lagging
behind population growth on an annual basis.
• The pool of potential workers is generally becoming older, with the Baby Boomers
increasingly reaching pension age and beyond, and with migrant arrivals tending to be an

older cohort than we saw pre-Covid – more in their 30s and 40s than their 20s.
• Net migration flows have started to pick up from their lows since late 2025. Departures of
New Zealanders have come down from their highs, though they remain above pre-Covid
levels, as the relative appeal of the Australian jobs market has narrowed.
• Foreign arrivals have picked up to some degree, although the growth appears to have been
mostly driven by students rather than those on work visas.

### Page 8

Westpac New Zealand Economics
8
Sectoral and regional trends
• Jobs growth has been relatively strong in government-connected sectors such as healthcare
and education, as well as in local and central government administration.
• Mining and utilities have seen strong growth in percentage terms, but neither of these
sectors are big employers in absolute terms.
• The construction sector is now starting to see a lift in employment, after a steep decline

over 2024-25. Residential building consents have risen strongly over the last year or so,
pointing to a growing pipeline of work for the homebuilding industry.
• Sectors that are connected to international tourism, such as transport and recreational
services, had previously seen strong employment growth, but this appears to have flattened
out in recent months.
• Employment trends have generally been more positive in the South Island than the North,

which likely reflects the greater influence of the agricultural sector – while not a large
employer in itself, farmers have benefited from high export prices and their spending has
flowed into other sectors.

Michael Gordon, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5670 | michael.gordon@westpac.co.nz      Connect with us

### Page 9

Westpac New Zealand Economics
9
Wages
• The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September quarter. Annual growth
remained around the lowest since 2021.
• While the overall result was in line with our forecast, the details were a little weaker than
we expected, with less contribution from private sector wages (up 0.5%) and a surprisingly
large 0.7% increase in the public sector.
• The distribution of pay rates has returned to pre-Covid trends. Only 44% of roles saw a

pay rise over the last year; of those roles that did see an increase, the average size has
converged on the 2-3% range.
• The LCI is not a measure of what workers actually receive – it reflects the cost to businesses
for a given unit of labour – and hence shouldn’t be compared to the inflation rate. A more
suitable measure is the analytical unadjusted LCI, which includes pay rises that are related
to productivity gains. This measure slowed from 3.1% to 2.9% on an annual basis, the lowest

since March 2021.
• There was a marked pickup in the proportion of firms reporting cost-of-living adjustments as
the main reason for pay increases. However, the size of those increases varied greatly, with
many of them below the rate of CPI inflation.
• This seems to reflect a shift in the justification for pay rises, rather than an overall increase in
wage pressures – firms are more likely to cite cost-of-living adjustments because they’re less
likely to cite demand-side pressures.

### Page 10

Westpac New Zealand Economics
10
International comparisons
• New Zealand’s unemployment rate is the 18th lowest in the OECD (out of 38 countries). This
ranking has been broadly unchanged over the last year or so.
• Notably, New Zealand’s unemployment rate is substantially above Australia’s, whereas
historically it has been lower on average. This shift in relative job opportunities has been a
key attractor for New Zealanders over the last couple of years – as it was in the early 2010s

when the Australian economy was benefiting from a boom in mining construction.
• However, that gap between unemployment rates is no longer widening, with the Australian
jobs market starting to soften this year as the RBA has raised interest rates to rein in inflation
(which was already running too high before the Iran conflict and the surge in oil prices). We
expect Australia’s unemployment to rise further over the year ahead.
• New Zealand has the 7th highest employment rate in the OECD (down from 6th last quarter).

We tend to have high rates of labour force participation for 15-64 year olds, and particularly
high participation for the over-65s, as the lack of means testing for NZ Superannuation
means there is no financial disincentive to working beyond pension age.

### Page 1

The Goldman Sachs Group, Inc.
Global bond yields have risen to multi-decade highs, yet growth and markets have
remained remarkably resilient. But with some pressures beginning to emerge (e.g. in
European sovereign bond markets), can this resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities
be revealed? We ask the IMF’s Tobias Adrian, Moody’s Analytics’ Mark Zandi, and GS
economists. Adrian is relatively sanguine about the economy’s resilience, though
notes downside risks, while GS economists expect only a modest growth drag from

higher rates, but a more consequential one if yields remain near current or higher
levels. Zandi is generally more concerned, describing the US economy as vulnerable
rather than resilient, and sees one-in-three odds of the economy going off the r ails.
Meanwhile, GS strategists and analysts are not especially concerned about banks or
markets more broadly, but warn that further increases in yields or rate volatility could
test risk assets. Key to watch: whether the increasingly interconnected and leveraged

AI investment boom can continue to withstand higher rates.
Tobias Adrian, Special Advisor and former Director of Monetary and Capital Markets, IMF
“The global economy has demonstrated considerable resilience in the face of multiple severe shocks, and I would not be
surprised if it continues to shrug off higher yields, pr ovided they do not keep rising sharply in a disorderly fashion. At the same
time, I do worry about downside risks.”
Mark Zandi, Chief Economist, Moody’s Analytics

We provide a brief snapshot on the most important economies for the global markets

### Page 3

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 3 
Top of Mind Issue 152

Recent acceleration in the rise in global bond yields to multi-
decade highs has begun to raise questions about the 
implications for growth and markets. Both have remained 
remarkably resilient so far, with US growth in particular 
holding relatively firm and the S&P 500 and other equity 
markets near new all-time highs. But with pressure 
beginning to emerge—most notably in European sovereign 
bond markets—and yields looking unlikely to retreat soon, 
can that resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities be revealed? 
GS Head of US Rates Strategy William Marshall first lays out 
how we arrived at higher rates, underscoring the striking 
breadth of the rise, which he attributes mainly to 
fundamentals—strong growth, inflation risks, hawkish policy 
shifts, the ongoing investment cycle, and long-run fiscal 
risks—but notes that technical factors likely amplified the 
recent move. And while he ultimately expects yields to fall 
(with YE 10-year US Treasury yields of 4.75%/4.4% in 
2026/2027, respectively) as markets become more confident 
in easing inflation, he thinks elevated long-term yields should 
have some staying power, and sees continued upside risk 
from energy price and fiscal pressures.  
So, just how much of a threat do higher rates pose to the 
macro outlook? For answers, we first turn to Tobias Adrian, 
Special Advisor to the Managing Director and former 
Financial Counsellor and Director of the Monetary and Capital 
Markets Department at the IMF, Mark Zandi, Chief 
Economist of Moody’s Analytics, and GS US economists. 
Adrian sees a global economy that has so far responded to 
the yield shock much as it has to the many other major 
shocks of the past seven years: resiliently. He notes that the 
rise in yields has not significantly tightened financial 
conditions or, more importantly, disrupted market functioning 
or forced damaging deleveraging. He believes that resilience 
could persist as long as the move in yields doesn’t accelerate 
or become more disorderly. But he is concerned about 
vulnerabilities in the AI and technology complex, sovereign 
bond markets, and EMs—not to mention cyber risks—all of 
which leaves him “vigilant but cautiously optimistic.” 
GS US economists also characterize the US economy as 
resilient. Their expectation that the Fed will deliver just one 
more rate hike this year before cutting next year—consistent 
with Marshall’s expectation of lower Treasury yields—leads 
Pierfrancesco Mei to expect only a modest 0.2pp growth 
drag in 2027 from higher rates. But he finds that the drag 
could rise to a more consequential 0.5pp if yields remain near 
current levels (see pg. 9 for GS global economists Joseph 
Briggs’ and Johan Allen’s rules of thumb for the impact of 
higher yields on rate-sensitive areas across major DMs.) 
David Mericle and Mei also worry about the implications for 
the already-concerning US fiscal trajectory, which they 
believe is unlikely to trigger a classic debt crisis but is too risky 
to ignore.     
Zandi, for his part, is generally more concerned. While his 
baseline outlook is relatively sanguine, he describes the US 
economy as vulnerable rather than resilient and views the 
“distribution of possible outcomes as very flat and heavily 
skewed toward darker tail scenarios.” He sees the clearest 
softness in the labor market, with weak hiring and falling real 
wage growth for many workers weighing on consumer 
spending. And he takes little comfort from US growth 
continuing to run around 2%—roughly its potential rate—
noting that AI accounts for roughly one-third of that growth, 
leaving the non-AI economy growing at only around 1-1.5%, 
which he calls “a pretty punk economy.” Zandi also worries 
that the US fiscal situation is “as bad as I have ever seen it” 
and that higher rates are already inflicting damage on real 
estate markets and squeezing existing debtors. As these 
stresses mount, he sees elevated risk of something breaking 
in the financial system. All told, he puts the odds of the 
current vulnerabilities pushing the economy completely off 
the rails at around one-in-three.  
With banks historically at the center of the rate shocks and 
economic crises that Zandi is worried about, we then explore 
how much risk banks pose today. Adrian and GS Head of the 
Financials Equity Group Richard Ramsden both see little 
cause for alarm, explaining that higher rates should boost 
banks’ net interest income and revenues given their asset-
sensitive balance sheets. Ramsden also doesn’t expect a 
significant deterioration in credit quality absent a broader 
economic downturn and says that most banks should have 
excess capital even after accounting for rate-driven portfolio 
losses.   
But even if banks can weather higher rates, what about 
markets more broadly? GS strategists and analysts are not 
especially concerned, with Chief US Equity Strategist Ben 
Snider arguing that current rate levels shouldn’t prevent 
continued strong US earnings growth and the S&P 500 from 
climbing higher, Chief Credit Strategist Amanda Lynam 
viewing the headwind to corporate credit from higher rates 
as manageable amid the solid growth backdrop, and US 
Financials Analyst Alexander Blostein expecting the recent 
slowdown in private market activity to rebound once rates 
stabilize. GS multi-asset strategists Christian Mueller-
Glissmann and Andrea Ferrario add that higher yields have 
strengthened the strategic case for bonds in a multi-asset 
portfolio, though strong earnings continue to favor equities 
for now. But they all warn that if rate volatility remains 
elevated, and especially if yields continue to rise from here, 
risk assets could face mounting pressures.  
All that said, with the AI boom powering the economy and 
markets, everything may come down to whether higher rates 
will derail it. Snider views this as unlikely given the strong 
balance sheets of the firms investing in AI and the expected 
profitability of these investments, with Lynam also seeing a 
high bar to derail AI-related debt issuance. Adrian also 
maintains a positive baseline for the AI economy but is 
concerned about the circularity of investment, increased 
leverage, and the sustainability of revenue growth in the AI 
complex. And Zandi again seems the most worried, warning 
that “AI could become the part of the economy that breaks” 
if rates remain at current levels for much longer.  
Allison Nathan, Editor

Tel: 212-357-7504

Higher bond yields: how concerning?

### Page 4
